self-indulgent#

Pronunciation
/ˌsɛlfɪnˈdʌldʒənt/ · self-in-DUL-juhnt
Русский
потакающий своим слабостям; самолюбующийся
Type
compound
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more self-indulgent, most self-indulgent
Form
compound — self + indulgent

Self-indulgent has 2 distinct senses.

1. Letting yourself have whatever you want, more than is good for you. (потакающий своим слабостям)

  1. A whole Saturday in bed felt gloriously self-indulgent.
  2. He has a self-indulgent streak when it comes to gadgets.

2. Too long or too pleased with itself, said of art and writing. (самолюбующийся, затянутый)

  1. The director's cut is self-indulgent at three and a half hours.
  2. Critics called the album more self-indulgent than his last one.

  3. Grief made her more self-involved than usual.

Contrast

semi-formal#

Pronunciation

Type

Position

Comparison
not gradable
Form

Contrast
American dictionaries prefer semiformal, with no hyphen; the two spellings are the same word.

Less formal than a black-tie event, but dressier than everyday clothes.

  1. The invitation says semi-formal, so wear a jacket.
  2. It was a semi-formal dinner at the club.
  3. We kept the tone of the meeting semi-formal.

Semiannual means twice a year, like biannual; biennial means every two years.

Happening twice a year.

  3. She goes in for a semiannual checkup.

Semitic#

Pronunciation
/səˈmɪtɪk/ · suh-MIT-ik
Русский
семитский
Type
origin
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Always capitalized. Semitic names a family of languages and peoples; anti-Semitic is used only of hostility toward Jews.

Belonging to the group of peoples and languages that includes Hebrew and Arabic.

  1. Hebrew and Arabic are Semitic languages.
  2. The inscription is written in a Semitic script.
  3. The course covers the Semitic peoples of the ancient Near East.

senior#

Pronunciation
/ˈsiːnjər/ · SEE-nyur
Русский
старший; пожилой; выпускной

Takes

Contrast
After senior use to, never than: She is senior to me, not senior than me.

Senior has 3 distinct senses.

1. Higher in rank, or longer in the job, than other people. (старший (по должности))

  1. She's a senior editor at the paper.
  2. Two more senior officers reviewed the case.
  3. He is senior to me by three years.

2. Older; meant for older people. (пожилой; для пожилых)

  1. Senior citizens ride the city bus for free.
  2. Ask about the senior discount before you pay.

3. Of the last year of high school or college. (выпускной (о последнем годе учёбы))

  1. My senior year was the best one.
  2. The senior class voted to go to Chicago.
  3. Senior prom is the third Friday in May.

Set has 3 distinct senses.

  1. He's set in his ways and won't try anything new.
  2. She's set on going to law school.

-ly adjective, from shape
Contrast
An adjective, though it ends in -ly: shapely legs, never she walked shapely.

Having an attractive shape.

  1. The dancer had long, shapely legs.
  2. A shapely vase stood on the mantel.
  3. The hedge is shapelier since the gardener came.

sharp#

Pronunciation
/ʃɑːrp/ · SHARP
Русский
острый; резкий; сообразительный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — sharper, sharpest
Opposite
blunt; dull
Contrast
Sharp is also an adverb in time phrases — at seven o'clock sharp — and in music a sharp note is slightly too high.

Sharp has 6 distinct senses.

1. Having an edge or a point that cuts easily. (острый (о ноже, крае))

  1. Careful — that knife is sharp.
  2. Use the sharpest scissors in the drawer.
  3. He cut his hand on a sharp piece of metal.

2. Sudden and large, of a change or a turn. (резкий (о повороте, изменении))

  1. There's a sharp turn at the bottom of the hill.
  2. The city saw a sharp rise in rents.
  3. Prices took a sharp drop in May.

3. Quick to notice or understand things. (сообразительный, острый (об уме))

  1. She still has a sharp mind at ninety.
  2. His sharp eyes caught the typo on page one.
  3. He's sharper than anyone else in the room.

4. Sudden and severe, of a pain. (резкий, острый (о боли))

  1. I felt a sharp pain in my side.
  2. A sharp stab of cold air hit us at the door.

5. Harsh and critical. (резкий, колкий (о словах, тоне))

  1. Her tone got sharp toward the end.
  2. There was a sharp exchange at the school board meeting.

6. Strong and slightly bitter, of a taste or smell. (острый, резкий (о вкусе, запахе))

  1. The recipe calls for sharp cheddar.
  2. The wine has a sharp finish.

Sickening has 2 distinct senses.

1. So bad or cruel that it shocks and disgusts you. (отвратительный, возмутительный)

  1. It was sickening to watch him blame the kids.
  2. The details that came out in court were sickening.
  3. There is something sickening about that much waste.

2. Making you feel physically ill. (тошнотворный)

  1. A sickening smell of gas filled the hallway.
  2. The car hit the guardrail with a sickening thud.

sickly#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɪkli/ · SIK-lee
Русский
болезненный; приторный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est or more/most — sicklier, sickliest; also more sickly, most sickly
Form

Opposite

Contrast
Despite the -ly, sickly is an adjective, not an adverb. It is not the same as sick: a sick child is ill today, a sickly child is ill again and again.

Sickly has 2 distinct senses.

1. Often ill and not strong. (болезненный, хилый)

  1. He was a sickly kid who missed half of third grade.
  2. The kitten was the sickliest of the litter.
  3. She looked pale and sickly after the flight.

2. Unpleasant in a weak, too-sweet or washed-out way. (приторный; нездорового оттенка)

  1. The waiting room was painted a sickly green.
  2. The punch had a sickly sweet taste.

Significant has 2 distinct senses.

  1. She gave her husband a significant look across the table.

silent#

Silent has 3 distinct senses.

1. Making no sound at all. (беззвучный, тихий)

  1. The house was silent except for the clock.
  2. The new dishwasher is almost completely silent.
  3. We stood in a silent line outside the door.

2. Saying nothing, especially about something people expect you to speak about. (молчаливый, умалчивающий)

  1. The company has been silent about the layoffs.
  2. He stayed silent for the whole meeting.

3. Not pronounced, or made without recorded sound. (немой (о букве, о фильме))

  1. The b in 'climb' is silent.
  2. She wrote her thesis on silent films.

Simulated has 2 distinct senses.

singular#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɪŋɡjələr/ · SING-gyuh-lur
Русский

Type

Position

Comparison
not gradable
Opposite

Contrast
The grammar sense is the everyday one. The 'remarkable' sense is formal and goes before a noun: a singular talent.

Singular has 2 distinct senses.

1. In grammar: naming one, not more than one. (единственного числа)

  1. 'Child' is singular and 'children' is plural.
  2. The verb has to agree with a singular subject.
  3. Singular 'they' is now standard in American writing.

2. Unusual and remarkable — formal. (исключительный, необыкновенный)

  1. She has a singular gift for calming a room.
  2. The building is a singular piece of American design.

situated#

  1. After the sale they were comfortably situated.

  1. That website looks sketchy — don't put your card in.
  2. We walked home through a sketchy part of town.
  3. The deal got sketchier the more he explained it.

2. Giving only a rough idea, without the details. (отрывочный, неполный)

  1. Details of the crash are still sketchy.
  2. My memory of that summer is pretty sketchy.

A word from American history, used about the South before the Civil War. The noun for the person is a slaveholder; writers today often prefer enslaver.

Owning enslaved people, or allowing people to be owned.

Position
both — mostly before a noun

Contrast
Slavic covers a language family and the peoples who speak it, so a Russian, a Pole and a Serb are all Slavic. Don't confuse it with slavish, which means copying without thought.

Of the Slavic peoples or their languages.

  1. Russian and Polish are both Slavic languages.
  2. She teaches Slavic literature at a state university.

slavish#

Pronunciation
/ˈsleɪvɪʃ/ · SLAY-vish
Русский

Type
evaluative
Position
attributive only — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more slavish, most slavish
Form

Contrast
Not the same as Slavic (of the Slavs), and the two are pronounced differently: SLAY-vish and SLAH-vik.

Slavish has 2 distinct senses.

1. Copying something exactly, with no ideas of your own. (слепо подражательный)

  1. The building is a slavish copy of a Paris train station.
  2. His writing is a slavish imitation of Hemingway.

2. Obeying or admiring someone far too much. (рабский, безоговорочный)

  1. The party demands slavish loyalty.
  2. She warned against slavish devotion to the rankings.

Pronunciation
/ˈslʌvənli/ · SLUV-uhn-lee
Русский
неряшливый, неопрятный
Type
evaluative
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more slovenly, most slovenly
Opposite
tidy
Contrast
Despite the -ly, this is an adjective, not an adverb. It has nothing to do with Slovenian.

Messy and careless about how you look or work.

  1. The sergeant called his uniform slovenly.
  2. She has slovenly habits in the kitchen.
  3. His desk is the most slovenly one on the floor.

slow#

Pronunciation
/sloʊ/ · SLOH
Русский
медленный; вялый; отстающий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — slower, slowest
Takes
to-infinitive — slow to answer
Preposition patterns
slow at
Opposite
fast
Contrast
The adverb is slowly: drive slowly. Drive slow and go slow are heard in speech and on signs, but write slowly.

Slow has 4 distinct senses.

1. Not moving quickly. (медленный)

  1. Traffic was slow the whole way home.
  2. She's the slowest runner on the team, and she still finished.

2. Taking a long time to happen or to get done. (долгий, затяжной)

  1. Recovery after the surgery was slow.
  2. He was slow to answer my email.

3. With little business or activity. (вялый, неоживлённый)

  1. Tuesday nights are slow at the restaurant.
  2. It's been a slow month for sales.

4. Of a clock or watch: showing a time earlier than the real one. (отстающий (о часах))

  1. The kitchen clock is five minutes slow.
  2. My watch runs slow in the cold.

slow-moving#

Pronunciation
/ˌsloʊˈmuːvɪŋ/ · sloh-MOO-ving
Русский
медленно движущийся; вяло идущий
Type
compound; also participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more slow-moving, most slow-moving
Form
compound — adverb + -ing participle

Slow-moving has 2 distinct senses.

1. Moving slowly. (медленно движущийся)

  1. A slow-moving storm is sitting over the valley.
  2. We were stuck behind a slow-moving tractor.

2. Going forward or selling slowly. (вяло идущий, залежавшийся)

  1. The store marked down its slow-moving inventory.
  2. It is a slow-moving case that has been in court for years.

Slow to understand things.

  3. He writes the villain as cruel and the henchman as slow-witted.

Sluggish has 2 distinct senses.

1. Moving or working more slowly than it should. (вялый, заторможенный)

  1. The engine is sluggish on cold mornings.
  2. I feel sluggish if I skip breakfast.

2. With little growth or activity, about business. (вялый (о рынке, экономике))

  1. Home sales have been sluggish all spring.
  2. The economy looks more sluggish than it did in January.

small#

Pronunciation

Type

Position

Comparison

Opposite

Contrast
small is a plain measurement; little often adds feeling (a little cabin sounds fond). Use small after how: How small is it?

Small has 3 distinct senses.

1. Not big in size or amount. (маленький, небольшой)

  1. They live in a small apartment near the park.
  2. Do you have this shirt in a smaller size?

2. Young. (маленький (о ребёнке))

  1. She has two small children at home.
  2. I remember that song from when I was small.

3. Not important. (незначительный, мелкий)

  1. It's a small problem — we can fix it today.
  2. That is a small price to pay for a quiet street.

smart#

Pronunciation
/smɑːrt/ · SMAHRT
Русский
умный; нарядный; умный (о технике)
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — smarter, smartest
Opposite
dumb
Contrast
In American English the first meaning is intelligent; in Britain smart usually means well-dressed. The technology sense is not gradable — a thermostat is smart or it isn't.

Smart has 3 distinct senses.

1. Intelligent, quick to understand. (умный, сообразительный)

  1. She's the smartest person in the room and she knows it.
  2. That was a smart decision.

2. Of a machine: controlled by a computer and able to react on its own. (умный (об устройстве))

  1. We put in a smart thermostat last winter.
  2. The building runs on smart lighting that shuts itself off.

3. Neat and stylish in appearance. (элегантный, нарядный)

  1. He looked smart in a navy blazer.
  2. The restaurant asks for smart casual dress.

smoldering#

Smoldering has 2 distinct senses.

1. Burning slowly, with smoke but no flame. (тлеющий)

  1. Firefighters watered down the smoldering debris.
  2. A smoldering cigarette had started the whole thing.

2. Strong but held in, of a feeling. (затаённый, тлеющий (о чувстве))

  1. There was a smoldering resentment between the two brothers.
  2. She gave him a smoldering look across the table.

smooth#

Pronunciation
/smuːð/ · SMOOTH
Русский
гладкий; плавный; вкрадчивый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — smoother, smoothest
Opposite
rough

Smooth has 3 distinct senses.

1. Flat and even to the touch, with no bumps or lumps. (гладкий, ровный)

  1. The stones by the river are worn smooth.
  2. Beat the sauce until it's completely smooth.
  3. She ran her hand over the smooth tabletop.

2. Going along without jolts or problems. (плавный, без сбоев)

  1. We had a smooth flight to Denver.
  2. The move to the new system was smoother than anyone expected.

3. Charming and confident in a way you do not quite trust, of a person. (вкрадчивый, слащавый)

  1. He's a smooth talker — don't sign anything today.
  2. The salesman was a little too smooth for my taste.

Soft has 4 distinct senses.

3. Quiet; not loud. (тихий, негромкий)

  1. She answered in a soft voice.
  2. The music was soft enough to talk over.

4. Gentle; not strict enough. (мягкий, нестрогий)

  1. The judge is soft on first offenders.
  2. Don't be too soft with them or nothing gets done.

solid#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɑːlɪd/ · SAH-lid
Русский

Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more solid, most solid
Contrast

Solid has 4 distinct senses.

1. Hard and keeping its shape; not liquid or gas. (твёрдый)

  1. The lake was frozen solid by January.
  2. Water becomes solid at 32 degrees.

2. Strongly built and not likely to break. (прочный, крепкий)

  1. It's an old table, but it's solid.
  2. They built a solid stone wall around the yard.

3. Made of one material all the way through. (цельный, сплошной)

  1. The ring is solid gold.
  2. The door is solid wood, not veneer.

4. Dependable and good, without being brilliant. (надёжный, добротный)

  1. He gave a solid performance in the second half.
  2. The case rests on solid evidence.

/ˌsɑːpəˈrɪfɪk/ · sah-puh-RIF-ik
Русский
снотворный, усыпляющий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more soporific, most soporific

Making you sleepy.

  1. The lecture had a soporific effect on the whole class.
  2. He read the list in a soporific monotone.
  3. The heat in that room is soporific.

sordid#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɔːrdɪd/ · SOR-did
Русский
грязный, низкий, мерзкий
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more sordid, most sordid

Morally dirty — involving lies, greed or shabby behavior.

  1. The paper printed every sordid detail.
  2. It's a sordid story of money and lies.
  3. The whole business was more sordid than anyone expected.

sore#

Pronunciation
/sɔːr/ · SOR
Русский
больной, воспалённый; обиженный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — sorer, sorest
Takes
about + noun in the "upset" sense
Contrast
a sore point is a subject that upsets someone. Do not confuse sore (hurting) with sorry.

Sore has 2 distinct senses.

1. Painful, especially from use or infection. (больной, воспалённый)

  1. I woke up with a sore throat.
  2. My legs were sorer the day after the hike.
  3. Her shoulder is still sore from the fall.

2. Annoyed and hurt about something (informal). (обиженный, задетый)

  1. He's still sore about losing the bet.
  2. Don't be a sore loser.

sorrowful#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɔːrəfəl/ · SOR-uh-fuhl
Русский
печальный, скорбный
Type
emotion
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more sorrowful, most sorrowful
Form
-ful from sorrow
Contrast
sorrowful is literary; everyday American English says sad.

Full of deep sadness, or showing it.

  1. She gave me a sorrowful look and said nothing.
  2. The song is slow and sorrowful.
  3. His face was sorrowful but calm.

sought-after#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɔːt ˌæftər/ · SAWT-af-tur
Русский

Type
compound
Position

Comparison

Form

Wanted by many people, and therefore hard to get.

  1. He's the most sought-after photographer in town.
  2. These tickets are highly sought-after.
  3. It's a sought-after address on the north side.

sound#

Pronunciation
/saʊnd/ · SOWND
Русский
здоровый, крепкий; разумный; глубокий (о сне)
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — sounder, soundest
Contrast
Also a noun (a loud sound) and a verb (That sounds good). safe and sound is a fixed phrase.

Sound has 3 distinct senses.

1. In good condition; not damaged or ill. (крепкий, исправный, здоровый)

  1. The inspector said the roof is sound.
  2. The kids came back safe and sound.

2. Sensible and based on good reasoning. (разумный, обоснованный)

  1. That's sound advice.
  2. His plan is sounder than mine.

3. Deep and unbroken, of sleep. (глубокий, крепкий (о сне))

  1. She's a sound sleeper — the storm never woke her.
  2. He fell into a sound sleep on the couch.

sound asleep#

Pronunciation
/ˌsaʊnd əˈsliːp/ · sownd-uh-SLEEP
Русский
крепко спящий
Type
quality
Position
predicative only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
fixed phrase — sound here is an old adverb meaning "deeply"
Contrast
Only after a verb. Never "a sound asleep baby" — before a noun use a sleeping baby.

Deeply asleep and hard to wake.

  1. The baby was sound asleep by seven.
  2. Both kids were sound asleep in the back seat.
  3. He's sound asleep — don't wake him up.

Built so that sound cannot pass through.

  1. The studio is completely soundproof.
  2. They put in soundproof windows facing the highway.
  3. It's not a soundproof room, so keep your voice down.

soupy#

Pronunciation
/ˈsuːpi/ · SOO-pee
Русский
жидкий, как суп; густой (о тумане)
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — soupier, soupiest

Soupy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Too thin and watery, of food. (жидкий, как суп)

  1. The risotto came out soupy.
  2. Add more rice if it still looks soupy.

2. Thick and heavy, of fog or air. (густой, вязкий (о тумане, воздухе))

  1. We drove through soupy fog all morning.
  2. The August air was soupier than ever.

sour#

Pronunciation
/ˈsaʊər/ · SOW-ur
Русский
кислый; прокисший; недовольный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — sourer, sourest
Opposite
sweet
Contrast
go sour is also used of plans and relationships: The deal went sour.

Sour has 3 distinct senses.

1. Sharp in taste, like a lemon. (кислый)

  1. These cherries are too sour to eat raw.
  2. She likes sour candy.

2. Spoiled, of milk or cream. (прокисший)

  1. The milk has gone sour.
  2. There was a sour smell in the fridge.

3. Unfriendly and bad-tempered. (недовольный, кислый (о виде))

  1. He gave me a sour look.
  2. Her mood got sourer as the day went on.

soured#

Pronunciation
/ˈsaʊərd/ · SOW-urd
Русский
испортившийся; натянутый (об отношениях)
Type
participial
Position
both — usually before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from sour

Soured has 2 distinct senses.

1. Turned unfriendly or bitter, of a relationship or mood. (натянутый, испортившийся)

  1. Soured relations between the two countries dragged on for years.
  2. He left the company and a soured friendship behind him.

2. Turned sour, of milk or cream. (прокисший)

  1. A carton of soured milk sat on the counter.
  2. You can still bake with slightly soured milk.

south#

Pronunciation

Русский
южный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun (the South) and an adverb (drive south). For a region or its people use southern: southern cooking, not "south cooking".

On or facing the south side.

  1. Their office is on the south side of the building.
  2. A south wind brought warmer air.
  3. We parked by the south entrance.

Comparison

Contrast
An -ly word that is an adjective, not an adverb. A southerly wind blows from the south, but a southerly course goes toward the south.

From the south, or toward the south.

  1. A southerly wind kept the night warm.
  2. We took a more southerly route to avoid the snow.
  3. The island is the most southerly point of the country.

Farthest to the south.

  2. They reached the southernmost tip of the island at dusk.
  3. This is the southernmost vineyard in the state.

sovereign#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɑːvrən/ · SAH-vruhn
Русский
суверенный; верховный
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun — the sovereign is a king or queen.

Sovereign has 2 distinct senses.

1. Independent and governing itself, of a country. (суверенный, независимый)

  1. Ukraine is a sovereign state.
  2. They demanded sovereign control of their own waters.

2. Holding the highest power. (верховный)

  1. In a monarchy the king is sovereign.
  2. Congress holds sovereign authority over the budget.

Soviet#

Pronunciation
/ˈsoʊviət/ · SOH-vee-uht
Русский
советский
Type
origin
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Always capitalized. Soviet means 'of the USSR', which was more than Russia: a Soviet passport, but the Russian language.

Belonging to the Soviet Union, the country that existed from 1922 to 1991.

  1. My grandfather grew up in a Soviet apartment block.
  2. The museum has a large collection of Soviet posters.
  3. Both sides signed the treaty during the Soviet era.

spare#

Pronunciation
/spɛr/ · SPAIR
Русский
запасной, лишний; скупой, лаконичный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more spare, most spare
Contrast
Before a noun spare usually means 'extra' (a spare tire); after a verb it more often means 'plain' or 'lean' (the room was spare).

Spare has 3 distinct senses.

1. Extra, and kept in case you need it. (запасной, лишний)

  1. There's a spare key under the mat.
  2. Do you have a spare minute?
  3. We put the guests in the spare room.

2. Plain and simple, with nothing added. (скупой, лаконичный)

  1. The room was spare: a bed, a chair, a lamp.
  2. His prose is spare and fast.
  3. The new design is more spare than the original.

3. Thin, with no extra flesh. (худощавый, сухощавый)

  1. He was tall and spare.
  2. Years of running left her lean and spare.

sparing#

Pronunciation
/ˈspɛrɪŋ/ · SPAIR-ing
Русский
экономный, скупой (на что-л.)
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly after a verb
Comparison
more/most — more sparing, most sparing
Takes
with or of + noun; in + noun
Form
-ing from spare
Contrast
You will meet the adverb far more often: use it sparingly.

Using or giving only a little of something.

  1. She is sparing with praise.
  2. Be sparing with the salt.
  3. Sparing use of color makes the room feel calm.

sparkling#

Pronunciation
/ˈspɑːrklɪŋ/ · SPAR-kling
Русский
искрящийся; игристый
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more sparkling, most sparkling

Sparkling has 3 distinct senses.

1. Shining with many small points of light. (искрящийся, сверкающий)

  1. The lake was covered with sparkling ice.
  2. She wore a sparkling silver dress.
  3. His eyes were bright and sparkling.

2. With bubbles in it, said of a drink. (газированный, игристый)

  1. Would you like still or sparkling water?
  2. We opened a bottle of sparkling wine.

3. Lively and brilliant, said of talk or a performance. (блестящий, искромётный)

  1. She gave a sparkling performance as Juliet.
  2. The dinner was full of sparkling conversation.

special#

Pronunciation
/ˈspɛʃəl/ · SPESH-uhl
Русский
особенный, особый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more special, most special
Takes
to + person, for the 'important to someone' sense

Special has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not ordinary; better or more important than usual. (особенный, необычный)

  1. Today is a special day for our family.
  2. You don't need anything special, just a pen.
  3. That song is more special to me than any other.

2. Meant for one particular purpose or group. (специальный, особый)

  1. He gets special treatment because of his last name.
  2. The school has a special program for new arrivals.

Made or trained for one narrow job or subject.

specific#

Pronunciation
/spəˈsɪfɪk/ · spuh-SIF-ik
Русский
конкретный, определённый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more specific, most specific
Takes
to + noun, for the 'belonging to one thing only' sense
Preposition patterns
specific to
Opposite
general

Specific has 2 distinct senses.

1. Exact and clearly stated, not general. (конкретный, точный)

  1. Can you be more specific about the time?
  2. She left me specific instructions.
  3. I need a specific date, not 'sometime in May'.

2. Belonging to one thing or group and to nothing else. (свойственный только чему-л.)

  1. This rule is specific to New York.
  2. The disease is specific to one breed of dog.

Named or stated exactly, usually in writing.

  1. Return the form by the specified date.

  3. Payment is due within the specified period.

speculative#

Pronunciation
/ˈspɛkjələtɪv/ · SPEK-yuh-luh-tiv
Русский
предположительный; спекулятивный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more speculative, most speculative

Speculative has 2 distinct senses.

1. Based on guessing rather than on facts. (умозрительный, предположительный)

  1. The report is speculative at this point.
  2. That's a speculative explanation, nothing more.
  3. Her second theory is even more speculative than the first.

2. Done in the hope of a profit, with a real risk of losing the money. (спекулятивный, рискованный)

  1. He made a speculative bet on oil.
  2. Speculative buying pushed housing prices up.

  2. Their reply was speedier than I expected.
  3. He's a speedy little dog for his size.

spellbinding#

Pronunciation
/ˈspɛlˌbaɪndɪŋ/ · SPEL-byn-ding
Русский

Position

Form

Contrast
spellbinding is the thing that holds you; spellbound is how you feel.

So interesting that you cannot look away or stop listening.

  2. She told a spellbinding story about her escape.
  3. His voice on the radio was spellbinding.

Steady has 3 distinct senses.

  1. He finally found a steady job with benefits.

Dull, old-fashioned and much too serious.

  1. The bank has a stodgy image it is trying to shake.
  2. He is less stodgy than he looks in that suit.
  3. The magazine got stodgier after the new editor arrived.

Pronunciation

Type

Position

Comparison

Stout has 3 distinct senses.

1. Of a person: rather fat and solid — a gentler word than fat. (полный, дородный, плотный)

  1. A stout woman in a straw hat was selling tickets.
  2. He has gotten stouter since he retired.

2. Thick and strong, and not likely to break. (крепкий, прочный, толстый)

  1. Wear stout boots — the trail is rough.

straight#

Pronunciation
/streɪt/ · STRAYT
Русский
прямой; ровный; честный; подряд; неразбавленный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — straighter, straightest
Opposite
crooked
Contrast
Straight is also a very common adverb: Go straight ahead, Come straight home. And do not confuse it with strait (a narrow channel of water).

Straight has 5 distinct senses.

1. Going one way without bending or curving. (прямой, не изогнутый)

  1. Draw a straight line from here to the corner.
  2. She has long straight hair.
  3. This road is straighter than the old one through the hills.

2. Level, even, or hanging in the right position. (ровный, прямо висящий)

  1. Is the picture straight?
  2. Your tie isn't straight.

3. Honest and direct, with nothing hidden. (прямой, откровенный, честный)

  1. Just give me a straight answer.
  2. I'll be straight with you: we can't afford it.

4. One after another, with no break — always before a noun. (подряд, кряду)

  1. They have won five straight games.
  2. It rained for ten straight days.

5. Of a strong drink: with nothing added. (неразбавленный, чистый (о напитке))

  1. He drinks his whiskey straight.
  2. I'll take a straight bourbon, no ice.

3. Of a taste, smell or drink: intense, with a lot of flavor or alcohol. (крепкий, резкий, насыщенный)

  1. I like my coffee strong.
  2. There was a strong smell of gas in the kitchen.

4. Firmly felt or hard to argue with. (твёрдый, убедительный (о мнении, доводах))

  1. She has strong opinions about the schools.
  2. There is strong evidence that the drug works.

stuck#

Pronunciation

Stuck has 3 distinct senses.

  1. I got stuck with the whole bill.
  2. We're stuck with this couch until we can afford a new one.

subservient#

Pronunciation
/səbˈsɜːrviənt/ · suhb-SUR-vee-uhnt
Русский
раболепный, угодливый; подчинённый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more subservient, most subservient
Takes
to + noun

Too willing to obey, or kept in a lower position.

  1. He hated the subservient tone his coworkers used with the boss.
  2. The board became subservient to the CEO.
  3. She refuses to be subservient to anyone.

subsidiary#

Pronunciation
/səbˈsɪdiɛri/ · suhb-SID-ee-air-ee
Русский
вспомогательный, второстепенный; дочерний
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
to + noun
Contrast
Also a common noun: a subsidiary of the company.

Less important than the main thing, and connected to it.

  1. Money was a subsidiary concern at that point.
  2. She played a subsidiary role in the negotiations.
  3. These questions are subsidiary to the main one.

subsidized#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌbsɪdaɪzd/ · SUHB-si-dyzd
Русский
субсидируемый, дотационный
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from subsidize
Contrast
American spelling subsidized; British subsidised.

Paid for in part by a government or an employer, so it costs less.

  1. They live in subsidized housing.
  2. The bus passes are subsidized by the city.
  3. Subsidized school lunches keep a lot of kids fed.

substandard#

Pronunciation
/ˌsʌbˈstændərd/ · suhb-STAN-durd
Русский
некачественный, не отвечающий нормам
Type
evaluative
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
sub- + standard

Below the quality that is required or expected.

  1. The building went up with substandard materials.
  2. Tenants complained about substandard heating.
  3. The work was substandard and had to be redone.

substantial#

Pronunciation
/səbˈstænʃəl/ · suhb-STAN-shuhl
Русский
значительный, существенный; солидный
Type
quantity; also quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more substantial, most substantial
Contrast
Substantial is about size or solidity; substantive is about real content. A substantial meal fills you up; a substantive discussion gets somewhere.

Substantial has 2 distinct senses.

1. Large in amount or important enough to matter. (значительный, существенный)

  1. They made a substantial donation to the school.
  2. There's been substantial progress since March.

2. Solidly built, or big and filling. (солидный, основательный; сытный)

  1. We had a substantial breakfast before the hike.
  2. The desk is old but substantial.

substantive#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌbstəntɪv/ · SUHB-stuhn-tiv
Русский
существенный, содержательный
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more substantive, most substantive
Contrast
Not the same as substantial: substantive changes change the meaning, substantial changes are simply big.

Dealing with the real content of something, not the surface.

  1. The draft needs substantive changes, not just edits.
  2. For the first time they had a substantive discussion.
  3. Nothing substantive came out of the meeting.

substitute#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌbstɪtuːt/ · SUHB-sti-toot
Русский
замещающий, запасной
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: a substitute teacher
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun (find a substitute) and a verb (substitute butter for oil). As an adjective it only goes before a noun — you cannot say the teacher was substitute.

Taking the place of the usual person or thing.

  1. We had a substitute teacher all week.
  2. The coach put in a substitute goalie in the third period.
  3. The district keeps a list of substitute drivers.

Under the ground.

subtropical#

Of the warm regions just outside the tropics.

  1. South Florida has a subtropical climate.

suburban#

Pronunciation
/səˈbɜːrbən/ · suh-BUR-buhn
Русский
пригородный
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more suburban, most suburban
Contrast
Urban is the city itself, suburban the ring of towns around it, rural the countryside.

Suburban has 2 distinct senses.

1. In or of the suburbs, the residential areas outside a city. (пригородный)

  1. They moved to a suburban school district.
  2. Suburban traffic is worst around three o'clock.

2. Typical of the suburbs — comfortable, ordinary and a little dull. (обывательский, мещанский)

  1. The whole movie makes fun of suburban life.
  2. This street feels more suburban than the rest of the city.

subversive#

Pronunciation
/səbˈvɜːrsɪv/ · suhb-VUR-siv
Русский
подрывной; крамольный
Type
classifying; also evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more subversive, most subversive
Contrast
Also a noun for a person: the government called them subversives.

Quietly working against a government, a system or accepted ideas.

  1. The book was banned as subversive.
  2. Her comedy is quietly subversive.
  3. They were charged with subversive activity.

succeeding#

Pronunciation
/səkˈsiːdɪŋ/ · suhk-SEE-ding
Русский
последующий
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — before a noun: succeeding generations
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ing from succeed in its older sense, to follow
Contrast
Here succeeding means following, not doing well. Each succeeding year means each year after it.

Coming after, one after another.

  1. Each succeeding year the festival got bigger.
  2. Succeeding generations told the story differently.
  3. The rule was kept by every succeeding administration.

successive#

Pronunciation
/səkˈsɛsɪv/ · suhk-SES-iv
Русский
следующий один за другим, последовательный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: three successive wins
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Successive means one after another; successful means it worked. Three successive wins is three in a row.

Following one after the other without a break.

  1. It rained for five successive days.
  2. She won three successive elections.
  3. Each successive draft got shorter.

succinct#

Pronunciation
/səkˈsɪŋkt/ · suhk-SINGKT
Русский
краткий, лаконичный
Type
quality
Position

Comparison
more/most — more succinct, most succinct
Opposite
long-winded

Saying a lot in very few words.

  1. Her answer was succinct and clear.
  2. Keep the summary succinct — one page at most.
  3. The second draft is more succinct than the first.

succulent#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌkjələnt/ · SUHK-yuh-luhnt
Русский
сочный (о еде); суккулентный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more succulent, most succulent
Contrast
Also a noun for plants like aloe and jade: a shelf of succulents.

Succulent has 2 distinct senses.

1. Juicy and delicious, of meat or fruit. (сочный, аппетитный)

  1. The lamb was succulent and fell off the bone.
  2. We ate succulent peaches all through August.

2. Thick and fleshy, of a plant that stores water. (суккулентный, мясистый)

  1. Cactus and aloe are succulent plants.
  2. Succulent leaves hold water through the dry season.

Sudanese#

Pronunciation
/ˌsuːdəˈniːz/ · soo-duh-NEEZ
Русский
суданский
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ese from Sudan
Contrast
Like all -ese words it adds no -s for people: two Sudanese students. For one person say a Sudanese man, not a Sudanese.

Of or from Sudan, or its people.

  1. The Sudanese students meet on Fridays.
  2. Her father is Sudanese.
  3. Sudanese coffee is served with ginger.

sudden#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌdən/ · SUH-duhn
Русский
внезапный, неожиданный
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more sudden, most sudden
Contrast
The adverb is suddenly, never sudden. All of a sudden is a fixed phrase meaning suddenly.

Happening quickly and without warning.

  1. There was a sudden drop in temperature.
  2. A sudden noise woke everyone up.
  3. His death was sudden — nobody had time to say goodbye.

sufficient#

Pronunciation
/səˈfɪʃənt/ · suh-FISH-uhnt
Русский
достаточный
Type
quantity
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
for + noun; to-infinitive
Opposite
insufficient
Contrast
More formal than enough, and it goes before the noun: sufficient evidence, but enough evidence.

As much as is needed.

  1. There wasn't sufficient evidence to charge anyone.
  2. One cup of rice is sufficient for two people.
  3. The money was sufficient to cover the repairs.

suffocating#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌfəkeɪtɪŋ/ · SUH-fuh-kay-ting
Русский
удушающий; давящий
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more suffocating, most suffocating
Form
-ing from suffocate

Suffocating has 2 distinct senses.

1. So hot and airless that it is hard to breathe. (удушливый, душный)

  1. The bus was suffocating by noon.
  2. A suffocating heat hung over the city.

2. So controlling that you feel trapped. (давящий, не дающий вздохнуть)

  1. She found the relationship suffocating.
  2. It was a suffocating little town to grow up in.

Position

Comparison

Contrast
Also a noun for a person: a Sufi. The tradition itself is Sufism, the mystical side of Islam.

Of the mystical tradition within Islam.

  1. She teaches Sufi poetry in translation.

  3. We heard Sufi music at the festival.

sugar-coated#

Pronunciation
/ˈʃʊɡər ˌkoʊtɪd/ · SHUU-gur-koh-tid
Русский
покрытый сахаром; приукрашенный
Type
compound
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — noun sugar + participle coated

Sugar-coated has 2 distinct senses.

1. Covered with a layer of sugar. (в сахарной глазури, покрытый сахаром)

  1. Sugar-coated cereal is basically dessert.
  2. The pills are sugar-coated so kids will take them.

2. Made to sound nicer than it really is. (приукрашенный, подслащённый)

  1. He gave us the sugar-coated version of the story.
  2. Her report was sugar-coated, but the numbers were bad.

sugarless#

Pronunciation
/ˈʃʊɡərləs/ · SHUU-gur-luhs
Русский
без сахара
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: sugarless gum
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-less from sugar
Contrast
Sugar-free is the more common label in American stores; sugarless is mostly used for gum and candy.

Made without sugar.

  1. She chews sugarless gum all day.
  2. They sell a sugarless version of the syrup.
  3. Sugarless candy usually uses sorbitol.

sugary#

Pronunciation
/ˈʃʊɡəri/ · SHUU-guh-ree
Русский
сладкий, приторный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more sugary, most sugary

Sugary has 2 distinct senses.

1. Containing a lot of sugar, or too sweet. (сахаристый, слишком сладкий)

  1. The kids drink way too many sugary sodas.
  2. The sauce was thin and sugary.

2. Too sweet in the way someone speaks or smiles. (приторный, слащавый)

  1. She answered in a sugary voice that fooled nobody.
  2. He gave me a sugary smile and walked off.

suggestive#

Pronunciation
/səˈdʒɛstɪv/ · suh-JES-tiv
Русский
наводящий на мысль; двусмысленный, фривольный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more suggestive, most suggestive
Takes
of + noun
Preposition patterns
suggestive of
Contrast
Watch out for the second meaning: a suggestive remark is usually about sex, not about making a suggestion.

Suggestive has 2 distinct senses.

1. Making you think of something; hinting at it. (наводящий на мысль о чём-то)

  1. The symptoms are suggestive of the flu.
  2. The room had a smell suggestive of old books.

2. Hinting at sex. (двусмысленный, фривольный)

  1. The lyrics are pretty suggestive.
  2. He made a suggestive comment and the table went quiet.

suicidal#

Pronunciation
/ˌsuːɪˈsaɪdəl/ · soo-uh-SY-duhl
Русский
суицидальный; самоубийственный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more suicidal, most suicidal
Form
-al from suicide

Suicidal has 2 distinct senses.

1. Wanting to kill yourself. (склонный к самоубийству, суицидальный)

  1. He called the hotline because he was feeling suicidal.
  2. Suicidal thoughts are a reason to get help right away.

2. So dangerous or foolish that it could destroy you. (самоубийственный, гибельный)

  1. Raising prices now would be suicidal.
  2. It was a suicidal charge across open ground.

suited#

Pronunciation
/ˈsuːtɪd/ · SOO-tid
Русский
подходящий, годный (для)
Type
participial
Position
predicative only — after a verb, with to or for: well suited to the job
Comparison
not gradable — but almost always with well, better or ideally: better suited to the job
Takes
to or for + noun
Form
-ed from suit
Contrast
Suited needs well, better or ideally in front of it; plain He is suited sounds wrong. Suitable is the word that goes before a noun: a suitable candidate.

Right for a particular job, person or situation.

  1. She's well suited to this kind of work.
  2. The house isn't suited for a family of six.
  3. Of the three, he's the best suited to run the meeting.

sulky#

Pronunciation

Русский
надутый, обиженный
Type
emotion
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — sulkier, sulkiest

Silently angry and showing it, the way a child does.

  1. He's been sulky ever since we said no.
  2. She gave me a sulky look and went to her room.
  3. The longer we waited, the sulkier he got.

sullen#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌlən/ · SUH-luhn
Русский
угрюмый, хмурый
Type
emotion
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more sullen, most sullen
Contrast
Sullen is grown-up and heavy; sulky is childish. Neither one is solemn, which is serious without the anger.

Sullen has 2 distinct senses.

1. Quiet and bad-tempered, refusing to talk. (угрюмый, мрачно молчаливый)

  1. The boy sat sullen and quiet through dinner.
  2. She answered with a sullen shrug.

2. Dark and gloomy, of the sky or weather. (хмурый, пасмурный)

  1. A sullen gray sky hung over the lake.
  2. The morning was cold and sullen.

sultry#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌltri/ · SUHL-tree
Русский
знойный, душный; чувственный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — sultrier, sultriest

Sultry has 2 distinct senses.

1. Hot and humid, of the weather. (знойный, душный)

  1. It was a sultry August afternoon.
  2. August in Houston is sultrier than anything I grew up with.

2. Attractive in a slow, sexual way. (чувственный, томный)

  1. She has a sultry voice.
  2. He gave her a sultry look across the bar.

Sumerian#

Pronunciation

шумерский
Type
origin
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun for the people and the language. Not Sumatran, which is about the island of Sumatra.

Of the ancient people of southern Mesopotamia, or their language.

  1. The museum has a room of Sumerian tablets.

  3. They spent the semester on Sumerian myths.

summa cum laude#

Pronunciation
/ˌsʊmə kʊm ˈlaʊdə/ · suu-muh kuum LOW-duh
Русский
с наивысшим отличием (о дипломе)
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: a summa cum laude graduate
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Latin, 'with highest praise'. It is used even more often as an adverb after graduate: She graduated summa cum laude. The three levels, lowest first, are cum laude, magna cum laude, summa cum laude.

Graduating with the highest level of honors.

  1. The program takes mostly summa cum laude graduates.
  2. She put her summa cum laude honors right at the top of her résumé.
  3. He was the only summa cum laude student in his class that year.

summary#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌməri/ · SUH-muh-ree
Русский
суммарный, упрощённый (о процедуре)
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: summary judgment
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Far more common as a noun (write a summary). The adjective is legal or official and means done fast, skipping the usual steps: summary judgment, summary dismissal.

Done quickly and without the usual procedure.

  1. The judge granted summary judgment for the defense.
  2. He was fired in a summary dismissal with no hearing.
  3. The report described summary executions in the border towns.

Like summer, or right for summer.

  1. She wore a light, summery dress.
  2. It's unusually summery for April.
  3. The menu turns summery in June — lots of tomatoes and corn.

sunburned#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌnbɜːrnd/ · SUHN-burnd
Русский

participial
Position
both
Comparison

Form

Contrast
American English says sunburned; sunburnt is mostly British. Tanned is the brown you want, sunburned is the red you don't.

Red and sore from too much sun.

sundry#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌndri/ · SUHN-dree
Русский
разный, всякий
Type
quantity
Position
attributive only — before a noun: sundry items
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Formal or joking, and usually in the phrases various and sundry or all and sundry (everybody). The noun sundries means small odds and ends.

Of several different kinds, not worth listing one by one.

  1. The drawer is full of pens, cords and sundry junk.
  2. She handles billing, scheduling and sundry other tasks.
  3. Various and sundry relatives showed up for dinner.

sunken#

Pronunciation
/ˈsʌŋkən/ · SUHNG-kuhn
Русский
затонувший; впалый; углублённый
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — before a noun: a sunken ship
Comparison
not gradable
Form
the old participle of sink; the modern one is sunk
Contrast
Sunken only goes before a noun. After a verb you need sunk: a sunken ship, but the ship has sunk.

Sunken has 3 distinct senses.

1. Lying under the water after sinking. (затонувший, потопленный)

  1. Divers found the sunken ship in 300 feet of water.
  2. They mapped the sunken village under the reservoir.

2. Hollow, of eyes or cheeks. (впалый, ввалившийся)

  1. He had sunken cheeks and dark circles under his eyes.
  2. Her sunken eyes made her look exhausted.

3. Built lower than the floor or ground around it. (углублённый, заглублённый)

  1. The house has a sunken living room.
  2. There's a sunken garden behind the church.

Lit by the sun.

  1. We sat in a sunlit corner of the café.

  3. Dust floated in the sunlit air.

sunny#

Pronunciation

Sunny has 2 distinct senses.

1. With a lot of sunshine. (солнечный, ясный)

  1. It'll be sunny and warm all weekend.
  2. They have a sunny apartment on the top floor.

2. Cheerful and positive. (жизнерадостный, светлый)

  1. She has a sunny personality.

sunny-side up#

Pronunciation
/ˌsʌni saɪd ˈʌp/ · suh-nee-syd-UHP
Русский
глазунья, жаренная с одной стороны
Type
classifying
Position
after the noun — two eggs sunny-side up
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Only about fried eggs, and it follows the noun. The usual alternatives in a diner are over easy, over hard and scrambled.

Fried on one side only, so the yolk stays whole and runny.

  1. I'll have two eggs sunny-side up.
  2. He likes his eggs sunny-side up with rye toast.
  3. The plate came with hash browns and an egg sunny-side up.

super#

Pronunciation
/ˈsuːpər/ · SOO-pur
Русский
отличный, классный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Informal. Super is also used in front of other adjectives as an intensifier (super tired, super expensive) — that is an adverb, not this adjective. In a city apartment building, the super is the superintendent.

Very good indeed.

  1. We had a super time at the lake.
  2. That's super — thanks for setting it up.
  3. The food was super, and the service was fast.

Русский
стройный, изящный
Type
quality; also size
Position
both — a svelte dancer, She looked svelte
Comparison
more/most — more svelte, most svelte
Contrast
A complimentary word, used of people and of sleek objects; skinny and scrawny are the unkind ones.

Slim and elegant.

  1. She looked svelte in a plain black dress.
  2. The new laptop is svelte enough to disappear in a bag.
  3. He came back from the trip tanned and svelte.

swanky#

Informal and a little teasing — it hints that the money is on display. Elegant is the straight compliment.

Expensive and stylish in a showy way.

swarthy#

Pronunciation
/ˈswɔːrði/ · SWOR-thee
Русский
смуглый
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a swarthy sailor
Comparison
-er/-est — swarthier, swarthiest
Contrast
Mostly met in older fiction, and often stereotyping when used of people today; dark-skinned or olive-skinned is the neutral choice.

Having dark skin.

  1. The captain was a swarthy man with a gold tooth.
  2. In the novel every villain is swarthy and foreign.
  3. He was swarthier than his brothers.

Full of bold, romantic adventure, like an old pirate story.

sweeping#

Pronunciation
/ˈswiːpɪŋ/ · SWEE-ping
Русский
радикальный, широкий; огульный
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun: sweeping changes
Comparison
more/most — more sweeping, most sweeping
Form
-ing from sweep

Sweeping has 3 distinct senses.

1. Affecting a great many things; wide in scope. (радикальный, всеобъемлющий)

  1. The new law makes sweeping changes to the tax code.
  2. The changes are more sweeping than anyone expected.

2. Too general — made without looking at the details. (огульный, слишком общий)

  1. That's a sweeping generalization about an entire country.
  2. He made sweeping claims he couldn't back up.

3. Wide and curving, of a view or a line. (широкий, размашистый)

  1. The house has a sweeping view of the bay.
  2. A sweeping staircase curves up from the lobby.

sweet#

Pronunciation
/swiːt/ · SWEET
Русский
сладкий; милый, добрый; приятный
Type
quality; also evaluative
Position
both — a sweet apple, The tea is too sweet
Comparison
-er/-est — sweeter, sweetest
Takes
of + person in That's sweet of you; on + person, informal (He's sweet on her)
Opposite
sour, bitter (taste); dry (of wine)
Contrast
Sweet is the taste itself; sweetened means sugar was added. As a noun, sweets is candy; British English uses a sweet for dessert, American English says dessert.

Sweet has 3 distinct senses.

1. Tasting of sugar. (сладкий)

  1. The corn was sweet and fresh.
  2. This lemonade is too sweet for me.
  3. Georgia peaches are sweeter than the ones at the store.

2. Kind, gentle and lovable. (милый, добрый)

  1. What a sweet little kid.
  2. It was sweet of you to call.

3. Pleasant to smell, to hear or to experience. (приятный, сладостный)

  1. The sweet smell of cut grass came through the window.
  2. After that season, the win was especially sweet.

swell#

Русский

Type
evaluative
Position
both — a swell idea, That's swell
Comparison
not gradable — no sweller
Contrast
Dated and informal: you meet it in old movies and in dry sarcasm (Oh, that's just swell). Swell is also a verb (My ankle swelled up) and a noun (a big swell out past the reef).

Very good — an old-fashioned way of saying great.

  1. That's a swell idea.
  2. We had a swell time at the lake.
  3. Oh, swell — now the printer is broken.

swift#

Pronunciation
/swɪft/ · SWIFT
Русский
быстрый, стремительный; скорый
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a swift reply
Comparison
-er/-est — swifter, swiftest
Form
adverb swiftly
Opposite
slow
Contrast
Swift is more formal than fast and goes with actions and reactions more than with machines: a swift response, but a fast car. A swift is also a small bird.

Swift has 2 distinct senses.

1. Happening or done very quickly. (скорый, незамедлительный)

  1. The response from the city was swift.
  2. He gave a swift nod and walked out.

2. Moving fast. (быстрый, стремительный)

  1. The river is swifter here than it is downstream.
  2. A swift current pulled the boat sideways.

swollen#

Pronunciation
/ˈswoʊlən/ · SWOH-luhn
Русский
распухший, опухший; вздувшийся
Type
participial
Position
both — a swollen ankle, My hand is swollen
Comparison
more/most — more swollen, most swollen
Form
past participle of swell

Swollen has 2 distinct senses.

1. Bigger than normal because of injury or illness. (опухший, распухший)

  1. Her ankle was swollen and purple.
  2. He woke up with swollen glands.

2. Filled past its usual size, especially with water. (вздувшийся, разлившийся)

  1. The creek was swollen with snowmelt.
  2. Swollen rivers flooded the low fields.

sworn#

Pronunciation
/swɔːrn/ · SWORN
Русский

Type
participial
Position
attributive only — before a noun: sworn testimony, sworn enemies
Comparison
not gradable
Form
past participle of swear

Sworn has 2 distinct senses.

1. Given or made under oath. (данный под присягой)

  1. The lawyer read his sworn statement aloud.
  2. Only sworn officers can make the arrest.

2. Declared once and for good — of enemies and promises. (заклятый, клятвенный)

  1. The two families have been sworn enemies for years.
  2. He is a sworn opponent of the whole program.

sycophantic#

Pronunciation
/ˌsɪkəˈfæntɪk/ · sik-uh-FAN-tik
Русский
льстивый, подхалимский
Type
evaluative
Position
both — mostly before a noun: sycophantic praise
Comparison
more/most — more sycophantic, most sycophantic
Form
-ic from sycophant
Contrast
A false friend: Russian сикофант is a slanderer or informer, while English sycophant is a подхалим — someone who flatters a powerful person.

Praising a powerful person in an insincere way to get something.

  1. The interview was sycophantic from the first question.
  2. He's surrounded by sycophantic aides who agree with everything.
  3. Her review of the album is embarrassingly sycophantic.

symbiotic#

Pronunciation
/ˌsɪmbaɪˈɑːtɪk/ · sim-by-AH-tik
Русский
симбиотический; взаимовыгодный
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a symbiotic relationship
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ic from symbiosis

Symbiotic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Describing two living things that live together and help each other. (симбиотический)

  1. Clownfish and anemones have a symbiotic relationship.
  2. The fungus forms a symbiotic bond with the tree roots.

2. Describing two people or groups who each need the other. (взаимовыгодный, взаимозависимый)

  1. Reporters and campaigns have a symbiotic relationship.
  2. The two companies grew in a symbiotic way.

symbolic#

Pronunciation
/sɪmˈbɑːlɪk/ · sim-BAH-lik
Русский
символический
Type
classifying
Position
both — a symbolic gesture, The dove is symbolic of peace
Comparison
more/most — more symbolic, most symbolic
Takes
of + noun (what it stands for)
Form
-ic from symbol

Symbolic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Standing for something else. (символизирующий, символический)

  1. The dove is symbolic of peace.
  2. Every color in the painting is symbolic.

2. Done to make a point rather than to have a real effect. (символический, формальный)

  1. The fine was symbolic — twenty dollars.
  2. It was a symbolic vote with no legal force.

Having two halves that match exactly.

Of or like a symphony and the large orchestra that plays one.

symptomatic#

Pronunciation
/ˌsɪmptəˈmætɪk/ · simp-tuh-MAT-ik
Русский
симптоматичный; с симптомами
Type
classifying

Comparison

Preposition patterns

Form
-ic from symptom
Opposite

Symptomatic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Being a sign of a larger problem. (симптоматичный, показательный)

  1. The delays are symptomatic of a bigger management problem.
  2. Empty storefronts are symptomatic of the whole town's decline.

2. Showing the symptoms of a disease. (с проявившимися симптомами)

  1. Only symptomatic patients were tested at first.
  2. She tested positive but never became symptomatic.

Form

Contrast
American spelling is -ized; synchronised is British. The Olympic sport is officially artistic swimming now, but people still say synchronized swimming.

Made to happen at the same time or at the same speed.

  1. The audio and the video aren't synchronized.
  2. She swam on a synchronized swimming team in college.
  3. All the traffic lights on Main are synchronized.

Having the musical accent on the off beats instead of the strong ones.

synonymous#

Pronunciation
/sɪˈnɑːnɪməs/ · si-NAH-nuh-muhs
Русский
синонимичный; неразрывно связанный
Type
classifying
Position
both — almost always after a verb, with with
Comparison
more/most — more synonymous, most synonymous
Takes
with + noun
Preposition patterns
synonymous with
Form
-ous from synonym

Synonymous has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having the same meaning as another word. (синонимичный)

  1. Big and large are nearly synonymous.
  2. The two terms are synonymous in everyday speech.

2. So closely connected that one makes you think of the other. (неразрывно связанный, ставший символом)

  1. His name became synonymous with the city itself.
  2. For my grandparents, Detroit was synonymous with cars.

synthetic#

Pronunciation
/sɪnˈθɛtɪk/ · sin-THET-ik
Русский
синтетический, искусственный; неискренний
Type
material; also classifying
Position
both — a synthetic fabric, The fibers are synthetic
Comparison
not gradable — but more synthetic is possible about a sound or a feeling
Form
-ic from synthesis
Opposite
natural

Synthetic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Made by a chemical process instead of coming from nature. (синтетический, искусственный)

  1. The jacket is filled with a synthetic material.
  2. Synthetic rubber holds up better in the cold.
  3. Most of the vitamins on that shelf are synthetic.

2. Not genuine — made to look or sound real. (искусственный, поддельный)

  1. The dialogue felt synthetic.
  2. There was something synthetic about his enthusiasm.

systematic#

Pronunciation
/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/ · sis-tuh-MAT-ik
Русский
систематический, методичный
Type
quality; also classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a systematic search
Comparison
more/most — more systematic, most systematic
Form
-ic from system
Opposite
haphazard, random
Contrast
Systematic means done step by step, on a plan; systemic means built into the whole system: a systematic search but systemic corruption.

Done carefully, in an organized, step-by-step way.

  1. They made a systematic search of the building.
  2. We need a more systematic way to track orders.
  3. The report describes systematic abuse over many years.

systemic#

Pronunciation
/sɪˈstɛmɪk/ · si-STEM-ik
Русский
системный; общий (о всём организме)
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: systemic failure
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ic from system
Contrast
Don't mix it up with systematic, which means methodical. Systemic means running through the whole system: systemic racism, a systemic infection.

Systemic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Affecting a whole organization or society, not just one part of it. (системный)

  1. The audit found systemic problems, not one bad manager.
  2. They call it a systemic failure of oversight.

2. Affecting the entire body, in medicine. (общий, системный (о болезни))

  1. The infection became systemic.
  2. Systemic drugs travel through the bloodstream.
